It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired or like you don’t have the energy to do things, even things that wouldn’t usually bother you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m, like, collapsing or anything, but I do feel worn out more than makes sense. Even on a day where I haven’t done much, my body feels like I already put in a full shift.\n\nLike with work, helping a client shower or just doing the normal stuff I’ve done a hundred times, it takes more out of me. Or carrying groceries in from the car—usually that’s nothing, but lately I get inside and I’m like, why am I so tired from that? It’s frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any trouble concentrating or making decisions lately, even about small things?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s kind of the main thing, honestly. My head feels foggy, like I’m trying to think through cotton or something.\n\nI’ll be doing my client notes and then later notice little mistakes—nothing huge, but enough that I’m like, come on, I know better than that. Or I’ll walk into a room to grab something and just stand there thinking, what was I even doing? Which is annoying, because I’m usually pretty good at keeping track of things. Like at work you have to be, you know? One person needs this, another needs that, and if you miss a detail… anyway, yeah, concentrating has definitely been harder lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to make decisions, even about small things, or felt like your thoughts are slowed down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It’s more like my thoughts feel… foggy, you know? Like I’m not stupid, I know how to do my job, but lately I’m making these little mistakes in my client notes, stuff I’d normally catch right away.\n\nAnd I’ll go to do something—like walk into the kitchen, or go grab something for a client—and then I’m standing there like, wait, what was I doing? Which is frustrating, because I’m usually pretty on top of details. I mean, in my line of work you kind of have to be. Even little things matter. So yeah, that’s honestly part of what made me come in. My head just doesn’t feel as clear as it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t bring you the same pleasure they used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed that exactly. Like I still like the things I like, I think.\n\nI’m not a huge “hobby” guy anyway, unless you count watching old game shows or messing around with stuff in the garage when I get around to it. And with friends, it depends—everybody’s busy, you know? My one buddy works nights, and then scheduling anything turns into this whole puzzle.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought, like, “oh, this doesn’t make me happy anymore.” It’s more just… I don’t know, hard to tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to look forward to, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with people, just don’t feel as rewarding or enjoyable lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t know if I’d say they’re not enjoyable. My uncle used to call those shows “old people TV,” which is funny because now I’m the one watching them.\n\nBut, yeah, I don’t know. I’m honestly not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a big change like that. Like, I still like the idea of doing those things, but half the time getting to them is the issue.

One person needs this, another needs that, and if you miss a detail… anyway, yeah, concentrating has definitely been harder lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to make decisions, even about small things, or felt like your thoughts are slowed down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It’s more like my thoughts feel… foggy, you know? Like I’m not stupid, I know how to do my job, but lately I’m making these little mistakes in my client notes, stuff I’d normally catch right away.\n\nAnd I’ll go to do something—like walk into the kitchen, or go grab something for a client—and then I’m standing there like, wait, what was I doing? Which is frustrating, because I’m usually pretty on top of details. I mean, in my line of work you kind of have to be. Even little things matter. So yeah, that’s honestly part of what made me come in. My head just doesn’t feel as clear as it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t bring you the same pleasure they used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed that exactly. Like I still like the things I like, I think.\n\nI’m not a huge “hobby” guy anyway, unless you count watching old game shows or messing around with stuff in the garage when I get around to it. And with friends, it depends—everybody’s busy, you know? My one buddy works nights, and then scheduling anything turns into this whole puzzle.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought, like, “oh, this doesn’t make me happy anymore.” It’s more just… I don’t know, hard to tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to look forward to, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with people, just don’t feel as rewarding or enjoyable lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t know if I’d say they’re not enjoyable. Like if I put on one of my shows, I still watch it, but half the time I’m also checking my phone or thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer, and then I miss the part where someone wins the bonus round. My uncle used to call those shows “old people TV,” which is funny because now I’m the one watching them.\n\nBut, yeah, I don’t know. I haven’t really sat there thinking, “this doesn’t make me happy anymore.” Spending time with people is hard to judge too, because coordinating schedules is a whole thing. So I’m not sure I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you used to really look forward to, like certain activities or time with people, just don’t feel as satisfying or rewarding lately?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a big change like that. Like, I still like the idea of doing those things, but half the time getting to them is the issue.\n\nLike my cousin keeps trying to get me to come over for cards, and then it turns into who’s driving, what time, is his kid’s soccer thing running late—there’s always some complication. So by the time it happens, I’m already kind of over the planning part. But once I’m there, I don’t know, it’s fine.\n\nSo… I guess I can’t really say they don’t feel satisfying. I’m not really sure I can say that, no. Like… I haven’t been keeping score in my head, you know? If I watch something or talk to somebody, it’s not like I’m sitting there measuring whether it feels the same as it did before.

My TV remote has been acting up too, which sounds unrelated, but it makes watching anything annoying because you have to press the button three times and then it skips too far—so then I’m irritated at the remote, not the show.

My head feels foggy, like I’m trying to think through cotton or something.\n\nI’ll be doing my client notes and then later notice little mistakes—nothing huge, but enough that I’m like, come on, I know better than that. Or I’ll walk into a room to grab something and just stand there thinking, what was I even doing? Which is annoying, because I’m usually pretty good at keeping track of things. Like at work you have to be, you know? One person needs this, another needs that, and if you miss a detail… anyway, yeah, concentrating has definitely been harder lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to make decisions, even about small things, or felt like your thoughts are slowed down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It’s more like my thoughts feel… foggy, you know? Like I’m not stupid, I know how to do my job, but lately I’m making these little mistakes in my client notes, stuff I’d normally catch right away.\n\nAnd I’ll go to do something—like walk into the kitchen, or go grab something for a client—and then I’m standing there like, wait, what was I doing? Which is frustrating, because I’m usually pretty on top of details. I mean, in my line of work you kind of have to be. Even little things matter. So yeah, that’s honestly part of what made me come in. My head just doesn’t feel as clear as it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t bring you the same pleasure they used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed that exactly. Like I still like the things I like, I think.\n\nI’m not a huge “hobby” guy anyway, unless you count watching old game shows or messing around with stuff in the garage when I get around to it. And with friends, it depends—everybody’s busy, you know? My one buddy works nights, and then scheduling anything turns into this whole puzzle.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought, like, “oh, this doesn’t make me happy anymore.” It’s more just… I don’t know, hard to tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to look forward to, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with people, just don’t feel as rewarding or enjoyable lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t know if I’d say they’re not enjoyable. Like if I put on one of my shows, I still watch it, but half the time I’m also checking my phone or thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer, and then I miss the part where someone wins the bonus round. My uncle used to call those shows “old people TV,” which is funny because now I’m the one watching them.\n\nBut, yeah, I don’t know. I haven’t really sat there thinking, “this doesn’t make me happy anymore.” Spending time with people is hard to judge too, because coordinating schedules is a whole thing. So I’m not sure I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you used to really look forward to, like certain activities or time with people, just don’t feel as satisfying or rewarding lately?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a big change like that. Like, I still like the idea of doing those things, but half the time getting to them is the issue.\n\nLike my cousin keeps trying to get me to come over for cards, and then it turns into who’s driving, what time, is his kid’s soccer thing running late—there’s always some complication. So by the time it happens, I’m already kind of over the planning part. But once I’m there, I don’t know, it’s fine.\n\nSo… I guess I can’t really say they don’t feel satisfying. I just haven’t paid attention to it that way.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past few weeks, have you noticed that things you used to really enjoy, or that used to make you feel good, just don’t seem to bring you the same feeling anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I can say that, no. I don’t know… I haven’t really been tracking it like that. I mean, I still talk to people, and if something’s funny I can laugh at it, but I’m not sure if it’s “less pleasure” or just me being distracted.

Like, my buddy was telling me about this barbecue place he found, and usually I’d be all into comparing sauces and whatever, but I was mostly thinking about whether I had laundry in the washer. Like I’m not stupid, I know how to do my job, but lately I’m making these little mistakes in my client notes, stuff I’d normally catch right away.\n\nAnd I’ll go to do something—like walk into the kitchen, or go grab something for a client—and then I’m standing there like, wait, what was I doing? Which is frustrating, because I’m usually pretty on top of details. I mean, in my line of work you kind of have to be. Even little things matter. So yeah, that’s honestly part of what made me come in. My head just doesn’t feel as clear as it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t bring you the same pleasure they used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve really noticed that exactly. Like I still like the things I like, I think.\n\nI’m not a huge “hobby” guy anyway, unless you count watching old game shows or messing around with stuff in the garage when I get around to it. And with friends, it depends—everybody’s busy, you know? My one buddy works nights, and then scheduling anything turns into this whole puzzle.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought, like, “oh, this doesn’t make me happy anymore.” It’s more just… I don’t know, hard to tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to look forward to, like watching your favorite shows or spending time with people, just don’t feel as rewarding or enjoyable lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t know if I’d say they’re not enjoyable. Like if I put on one of my shows, I still watch it, but half the time I’m also checking my phone or thinking about whether I left laundry in the washer, and then I miss the part where someone wins the bonus round. My uncle used to call those shows “old people TV,” which is funny because now I’m the one watching them.\n\nBut, yeah, I don’t know. I haven’t really sat there thinking, “this doesn’t make me happy anymore.” Spending time with people is hard to judge too, because coordinating schedules is a whole thing. So I’m not sure I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you used to really look forward to, like certain activities or time with people, just don’t feel as satisfying or rewarding lately?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a big change like that. Like, I still like the idea of doing those things, but half the time getting to them is the issue.\n\nLike my cousin keeps trying to get me to come over for cards, and then it turns into who’s driving, what time, is his kid’s soccer thing running late—there’s always some complication. So by the time it happens, I’m already kind of over the planning part. But once I’m there, I don’t know, it’s fine.\n\nSo… I guess I can’t really say they don’t feel satisfying. I just haven’t paid attention to it that way.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past few weeks, have you noticed that things you used to really enjoy, or that used to make you feel good, just don’t seem to bring you the same feeling anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I can say that, no. Like… I haven’t been keeping score in my head, you know? If I watch something or talk to somebody, it’s not like I’m sitting there measuring whether it feels the same as it did before.\n\nSome days I’m into it, some days I’m just kind of half-paying attention. But that might just be normal life. My TV remote has been acting up too, which sounds unrelated, but it makes watching anything annoying because you have to press the button three times and then it skips too far—so then I’m irritated at the remote, not the show.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’ve noticed things not feeling good anymore. I just don’t know that I’ve thought about it that directly.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you found yourself losing interest in most things, even ones you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say “most things,” no. I mean, I still do the usual stuff, and sometimes it’s fine. Like if somebody puts on a movie or I’m scrolling around looking at recipes I’ll never actually cook—my sister sends me these complicated ones with like twelve ingredients, and I’m like, who has saffron just sitting around?\n\nBut, sorry, the interest thing… I haven’t really noticed losing interest across the board. Some things feel like a hassle to start, maybe, but I don’t think I’ve just stopped caring about everything I usually like.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or sad most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like I’m crying all day or anything, but lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It’s hard to explain.\n\nI can still get through my shift and talk to people, you know, do the normal back-and-forth. Sometimes at work someone will tell me about their grandkids or what they watched on TV, and I can respond fine. I mean, I still talk to people, and if something’s funny I can laugh at it, but I’m not sure if it’s “less pleasure” or just me being distracted.\n\nLike, my buddy was telling me about this barbecue place he found, and usually I’d be all into comparing sauces and whatever, but I was mostly thinking about whether I had laundry in the washer. Which is a terrible example because I always forget laundry and then it smells weird if it sits too long.\n\nSo… I guess I can’t really say activities don’t bring me pleasure anymore. I haven’t noticed it clearly, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not really sure. I know you asked kind of the same thing, and I’m trying to answer it right, but I haven’t been sitting there thinking, like, “Was that rewarding?” you know?

I still do some of the stuff I usually do. Sometimes I enjoy parts of it, sometimes I’m just kind of there. Like with friends, if someone’s talking, I can follow along, and if it’s funny I’ll laugh. But then I’ll drift off thinking about some random thing, like whether I paid the electric bill or if I left a towel in the washer again.

So… I don’t know if I’d say it all stopped feeling enjoyable. I haven’t noticed that clearly.

Which is a terrible example because I always forget laundry and then it smells weird if it sits too long.\n\nSo… I guess I can’t really say activities don’t bring me pleasure anymore. I haven’t noticed it clearly, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you found that activities you used to really enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with people, just don’t feel as rewarding or enjoyable anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I guess I still don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. “No longer pleasurable” sounds so definite, and I’m not sure it’s like that.

I mean, I still show up when people ask me to, and I can have a decent conversation. Sometimes I’ll laugh at something or get into it for a minute. Then my mind sort of wanders off—like yesterday I was thinking about whether I bought dish soap, because I hate when you go to wash one fork and there’s just that little sad drop left in the bottle.

So… I haven’t noticed that everything I enjoy stopped feeling good. It’s more like I’m not paying close enough attention to say for sure.

So by the time it happens, I’m already kind of over the planning part. But once I’m there, I don’t know, it’s fine.\n\nSo… I guess I can’t really say they don’t feel satisfying. I just haven’t paid attention to it that way.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past few weeks, have you noticed that things you used to really enjoy, or that used to make you feel good, just don’t seem to bring you the same feeling anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I can say that, no. Like… I haven’t been keeping score in my head, you know? If I watch something or talk to somebody, it’s not like I’m sitting there measuring whether it feels the same as it did before.\n\nSome days I’m into it, some days I’m just kind of half-paying attention. But that might just be normal life. My TV remote has been acting up too, which sounds unrelated, but it makes watching anything annoying because you have to press the button three times and then it skips too far—so then I’m irritated at the remote, not the show.\n\nSo… I don’t think I’ve noticed things not feeling good anymore. I just don’t know that I’ve thought about it that directly.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you found yourself losing interest in most things, even ones you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say “most things,” no. I mean, I still do the usual stuff, and sometimes it’s fine. Like if somebody puts on a movie or I’m scrolling around looking at recipes I’ll never actually cook—my sister sends me these complicated ones with like twelve ingredients, and I’m like, who has saffron just sitting around?\n\nBut, sorry, the interest thing… I haven’t really noticed losing interest across the board. Some things feel like a hassle to start, maybe, but I don’t think I’ve just stopped caring about everything I usually like.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you felt down or sad most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like I’m crying all day or anything, but lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It’s hard to explain.\n\nI can still get through my shift and talk to people, you know, do the normal back-and-forth. Sometimes at work someone will tell me about their grandkids or what they watched on TV, and I can respond fine. But inside I just feel kind of sad and flat most of the day.\n\nSo… yeah, I guess that’s been there a lot these past couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you found that activities you used to enjoy, like hobbies or time with friends, don’t bring you the same pleasure they used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really been tracking it like that. I mean, I still talk to people, and if something’s funny I can laugh at it, but I’m not sure if it’s “less pleasure” or just me being distracted.\n\nLike, my buddy was telling me about this barbecue place he found, and usually I’d be all into comparing sauces and whatever, but I was mostly thinking about whether I had laundry in the washer. Which is a terrible example because I always forget laundry and then it smells weird if it sits too long.\n\nSo… I guess I can’t really say activities don’t bring me pleasure anymore. I haven’t noticed it clearly, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you found that activities you used to really enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with people, just don’t feel as rewarding or enjoyable anymore?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I know you asked kind of the same thing, and I’m trying to answer it right, but I haven’t been sitting there thinking, like, “Was that rewarding?” you know?\n\nI still do some of the stuff I usually do. Sometimes I enjoy parts of it, sometimes I’m just kind of there. Like with friends, if someone’s talking, I can follow along, and if it’s funny I’ll laugh. But then I’ll drift off thinking about some random thing, like whether I paid the electric bill or if I left a towel in the washer again.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d say it all stopped feeling enjoyable. I haven’t noticed that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two weeks, have you found that activities you once really enjoyed, like hobbies or spending time with people, no longer feel pleasurable or satisfying to you?\n\nPatient: I guess I still don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. “No longer pleasurable” sounds so definite, and I’m not sure it’s like that.\n\nI mean, I still show up when people ask me to, and I can have a decent conversation. Sometimes I’ll laugh at something or get into it for a minute. Then my mind sort of wanders off—like yesterday I was thinking about whether I bought dish soap, because I hate when you go to wash one fork and there’s just that little sad drop left in the bottle.\n\nSo… I haven’t noticed that everything I enjoy stopped feeling good. It’s more like I’m not paying close enough attention to say for sure."
